Small data global regularity for simplified 3-D Ericksen-Leslie’s compressible hyperbolic liquid crystal model
Jiaxi Huang 1, Ning Jiang 2, Yi-Long Luo 3, Lifeng Zhao 1

Abstract
. In this article, we consider the Ericksen-Leslie’s hyperbolic system for compressible liquid crystal model in three spatial dimensions. Global regularity for small and smooth initial data near equilibrium is proved for the case that the system is a nonlinear coupling of compressible Navier-Stokes equations with wave map to 𝕊 2 \mathbb{S}^{2} . Our argument is a combination of vector field method and Fourier analysis. The main strategy to prove global regularity relies on an interplay between the control of high order energies and decay estimates, which is based on the idea inspired by the method of space-time resonances. In particular the different behaviors of the decay properties of the density and velocity field for compressible fluids at different frequencies play a key role.



1.
Introduction

The hydrodynamic theory of liquid crystals was established by Ericksen
[
2
,
5
,
6
]
and Leslie
[
21
,
22
]
in the 1960’s (see also Section 5.1 of
[
27
]
). The compressible hyperbolic Ericksen-Leslie’s system was derived in Leslie’s paper
[
21
]
which consists of the following equations of the velocity field
u(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and the orientation field
d(x,t)\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
, and
(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
:

(1.1)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\rho+\mathrm{div}(\rho u)=0,\\
\partial_{t}(\rho u)+\mathrm{div}(\rho u\otimes u)+\nabla P=\mathrm{div}(\Sigma_{1}+\Sigma_{2}+\tilde{\sigma}),\\
\rho\ddot{d}=\Delta d+\Gamma d+\lambda_{1}(\dot{d}+Bd)+\lambda_{2}Ad.\end{array}\right.

In the above system,
P=P(\rho)
is the pressure, the superposed dot denotes the material derivative
\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla
, the symbol
\otimes
denotes the tensor product for two vectors with entries
(a\otimes b)_{ij}=a_{i}b_{j}
for
1\leq i,j\leq 3
, and

\displaystyle A=\tfrac{1}{2}(\nabla u+\nabla^{\top}u)\,,\quad B=\tfrac{1}{2}(\nabla u-\nabla^{\top}u)\,,

represent the rate of strain tensor and skew-symmetric part of the strain rate, respectively. We also define
N=\dot{d}+Bd
as the rigid rotation part of director changing rate by fluid vorticity. Here
A_{ij}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\partial_{j}u_{i}+\partial_{i}u_{j})
,
B_{ij}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\partial_{j}u_{i}-\partial_{i}u_{j})
,
(Bd)_{i}=B_{ki}d_{k}
, and
(\nabla d\odot\nabla d)_{ij}=\partial_{i}d_{k}\partial_{j}d_{k}
.
The notations
\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}
, and
\tilde{\sigma}
are as follows:

(1.2)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{1}:=\tfrac{1}{2}\mu_{1}(\nabla u+\nabla^{\top}u)+\mu_{2}\mathrm{div}u\ I,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{2}:=\tfrac{1}{2}|\nabla d|^{2}I-\nabla d\odot\nabla d,

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}_{ji}=\nu_{1}d_{k}d_{p}A_{kp}d_{i}d_{j}+\nu_{2}d_{j}N_{i}+\nu_{3}d_{i}N_{j}+\nu_{5}A_{ik}d_{k}d_{j}+\nu_{6}d_{i}A_{jk}d_{k}\,.

These coefficients
\nu_{i}(1\leq i\leq 6,i\neq 4)
which may depend on material and temperature, are usually called Leslie coefficients, and are related to certain local correlations in the fluid. Usually, the following relations are frequently used, see
[
2
,
21
,
34
]
.

(1.3)

\lambda_{1}=\nu_{2}-\nu_{3}\,,\quad\lambda_{2}=\nu_{5}-\nu_{6}\,,\quad\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}=\nu_{6}-\nu_{5}\,.

The first two relations are necessary conditions in order to satisfy the equation of motion identically, while the third relation is called
Parodi’s relation
, which is derived from Onsager reciprocal relations expressing the equality of certain relations between flows and forces in thermodynamic systems out of equilibrium. Under Parodi’s relation, we see that the dynamics of a compressible nematic liquid crystal flow involve five independent Leslie coefficients in (
1.2
). Furthermore, in (
1.1
), the Lagrangian multiplier
\Gamma
is (which ensures the geometric constraint
|d|=1
):

(1.4)

\Gamma=-\rho|\dot{d}|^{2}+|\nabla d|^{2}-\lambda_{2}d^{\top}Ad\,.

We remark that the last two terms in the third equation of (
1.1
) is the so-called kinematic transport, i.e.

(1.5)

g=\lambda_{1}(\dot{d}+Bd)+\lambda_{2}Ad\,,

which represents the effect of the macroscopic flow field on the microscopic structure. The material coefficients
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
reflect the molecular shape and the slippery part between the fluid and particles. The first term represents the rigid rotation of the molecule , while the second term stands for the stretching of the molecule by the flow. We remark that in
[
21
]
, Leslie also derived the incompressible model which can be written as:

(1.6)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla u-\tfrac{1}{2}\mu_{1}\Delta u+\nabla P=-\mathrm{div}(\nabla d\odot\nabla d)+\tilde{\sigma},\\
\mathrm{div}u=0\,,\\
\rho_{1}\ddot{d}=\Delta d+\Gamma d+\lambda_{1}(\dot{d}+Bd)+\lambda_{2}Ad\,,\end{array}\right.

where the Lagrangian multiplier
\Gamma=-\rho_{1}|\dot{d}|^{2}+|\nabla d|^{2}-\lambda_{2}d^{\top}Ad\,.
In the last equation of (
1.6
), the constant
\rho_{1}>0
is called the inertial constant. To distinguish them with their parabolic analogues, which have no
\ddot{d}
terms in (
1.1
) and (
1.6
), we call (
1.1
) and (
1.6
) Ericksen-Leslie’s hyperbolic system for compressible and incompressible liquid crystal, respectively.

As one of the important special cases, parabolic Ericksen-Leslie system (
\rho_{1}=0
,
\lambda_{1}=-1
) has been extensively studied since the mid 80’s. The static analogue of the parabolic Ericksen-Leslie’s system is the so-called Oseen-Frank model, whose mathematical study was initiated from Hardt-Kinderlehrer-Lin
[
11
]
. Since then there have been many works in this direction. In particular, the existence and regularity or partial regularity of the approximation (usually Ginzburg-Landau approximation as in
[
24
]
) dynamical Ericksen-Leslie’s system was started by the work of Lin and Liu in
[
24
]
,
[
25
]
and
[
26
]
. The simplest system preserving the basic energy law can be obtained by neglecting the Leslie stress and by specifying some elastic constants. In 2-D case, the existence of global weak solutions with at most a finite number of singular times were proved by Lin-Lin-Wang
[
23
]
. Recently, Lin and Wang proved global existence of weak solution for 3-D case with the initial director field lying in the hemisphere in
[
28
]
. For the general parabolic Ericksen-Leslie’s system, local well-posedness was proved by Wang-Zhang-Zhang in
[
32
]
, and in
[
12
]
existence of global solutions and regularity in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
was established by Huang-Lin-Wang. We refer to the references therein for more results for the parabolic Ericksen-Leslie’s system.

The study of hyperbolic Ericksen-Leslie’s system is at the infancy compared with its parabolic analogue. In hyperbolic Ericksen-Leslie’s system,
\rho_{1}>0
and (
1.1
) is a compressible Navier-Stokes equation coupled with a wave map with target
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. In one dimensional case, suppose
\tilde{\sigma}=0
and
u=0
, the system (
1.1
) can be reduced to a so-called nonlinear variational wave equation. Zhang and Zheng studied systematically the dissipative and energy conservative solutions
[
35
,
36
]
. For the multidimensional case, there has been some progress on the hyperbolic system of liquid crystal. Very recently, De Anna and Zarnescu
[
3
]
considered the inertial Qian-Sheng model of liquid crystals. They derived the energy law and proved the local well-posdedness for bounded initial data and global well-posedness under the assumptions that the initial data is small in suitable norm and the coefficients satisfy some further damping property. Furthermore, for the inviscid version of the Qian-Sheng model, Feireisl-Rocca-Schimperna-Zarnescu proved the global existence of the
dissipative solution
in
[
7
]
which is inspired from that of incompressible Euler equation defined by P-L. Lions
[
29
]
.

There are growing interest in the study of hyperbolic Ericksen-Leslie’s system. Recently, the second and third named authors of the current paper started the research of the well-posedness of Ericksen-Leslie’s hyperbolic system in the context of classical solutions: first for the incompressible model (
1.6
) in
[
16
]
and then with Tang for the compressible model (
1.1
) in
[
18
]
. More precisely, in
[
16
]
and
[
18
]
, under some natural constraints on the Leslie coefficients which ensure the basic energy law is dissipative, they showed the local-in-time existence and uniqueness of the classical solution to the system (
1.1
) with finite initial energy. Furthermore, with an additional assumption on the coefficients which provides a damping effect, i.e.
\lambda_{1}<0
, and the smallness of the initial energy, the unique global classical solution was established. Here we remark that both in
[
16
]
and
[
18
]
the assumption
\lambda_{1}<0
plays a crucial role in the global well-posedness. The limiting behaviors when the inertia constant
\rho_{1}\rightarrow 0
were considered in
[
19
,
20
,
17
]

When
\lambda_{1}=0
, which corresponds to the model without kinematic transport, the wellposedness is much more subtle analytically because there are no obvious damping effect. For incompressible model (
1.6
), Cai-Wang
[
1
]
recently made progress for the simplified Ericksen-Leslie system, namely, the case with
\nu_{i}=0,i=1\,,\cdots\,,6
,
i\neq 4
. They proved the global regularity of (
1.6
) near the constant equilibrium by employing the vector field method. In
[
13
]
, the authors of the current paper considered the more general incompressible model (
1.6
): still
\nu_{2}=\nu_{3}=0
, but
0\neq\nu_{5}=\nu_{6}>-\nu_{4}
, and
0\neq\nu_{1}>-2(\nu_{4}+\nu_{5})
. Of course, from (
1.3
), we still have
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=0
, i.e. the kinematic transport vanishes. They proved the global regularity for the more general case (but still special, compared with the most general case, say, (
1.2
) and (
1.3
)) near the constant equilibrium
(u,d)=(\vec{0},\vec{i})
. The argument of
[
13
]
is a combination of vector field method and Fourier analysis inspired by the so-called space-time resonance method.

In the current paper, we address the small data global regularity of the Ericksen-Leslie’s hyperbolic system for compressible model, i.e. (
1.1
). We consider the simplest case:
\nu_{1}=\nu_{2}=\nu_{3}=\nu_{5}=\nu_{6}=0
, hence
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=0
, and the pressure
P(\rho)
obeys the
\gamma
-law, i.e.
P(\rho)=a\rho^{\gamma}
with
\gamma\geq 1,a>1
. The aim of this paper is to prove the global regularity of this simplest case near the constant equilibrium
(\rho,u,d)=(1,\vec{0},\vec{i})
. More precisely, we study the Ericksen-Leslie’s hyperbolic liquid crystal model in the following form:
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}>0
,

(1.7)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\rho+\nabla\cdot(\rho u)&=0,\\
\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla u+\tfrac{\nabla P(\rho)}{\rho}&=\tfrac{1}{\rho}\big[\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}\Delta u+(\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}+\mu_{2})\nabla\mathrm{div}u+\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla|\nabla d|^{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\partial_{j}(\nabla d\cdot\partial_{j}d)\big],\\
\rho\ddot{d}-\Delta d&=(-\rho|\dot{d}|^{2}+|\nabla d|^{2})d.\end{aligned}\right.

on
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
with the constraint
|d|=1
. Compared with the incompressible model, the decay of the density
\rho(x,t)
and velocity field
u(x,t)
in compressible model is much more subtle.

1.1.
The main theorem

To state our main theorem, we need some notations. Define the perturbed angular momentum operators by

\tilde{\Omega}_{i}u=\Omega_{i}u+A_{i}u,\ \tilde{\Omega}_{i}d=\Omega_{i}d,

where
\Omega=(\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2},\Omega_{3})
is the rotation vector-field
\Omega=x\wedge\nabla
and
A_{i}
is defined by

(1.8)

A_{1}=e_{2}\otimes e_{3}-e_{3}\otimes e_{2},\ \ A_{2}=e_{3}\otimes e_{1}-e_{1}\otimes e_{3},\ \ A_{3}=e_{1}\otimes e_{2}-e_{2}\otimes e_{1}.

We define the scaling vector-field
S
by

S=t\partial_{t}+x_{i}\partial_{x_{i}}.

Let

\Gamma\in\{\partial_{t},\partial_{1},\partial_{2},\partial_{3},\tilde{\Omega}_{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{2},\tilde{\Omega}_{3}\}

and
Z^{a}=S^{a_{1}}\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}
, where
a=(a_{1},a^{\prime}):=(a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{8})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{+}^{8}
,
\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}=\Gamma^{a_{2}}\Gamma^{a_{3}}\cdots\Gamma^{a_{8}}
, we define

{\varrho}=\rho-1,\ {\varrho}^{(a)}=Z^{a}{\varrho},\ u^{(a)}:=Z^{a}u,\ \ d^{(a)}:=Z^{a}d.

The main result of this paper is as follows:

Theorem 1.1
.

Assume that
N_{0}:=60,N_{1}:=6,h:=6
, there is
\epsilon_{0}>0
, such that
(\rho_{0},u_{0},d_{0},d_{1})
are initial data near equilibrium
(1,\vec{0},\vec{i},\vec{0})
satisfying the smallness assumptions

(1.9)

\sup_{|a|\leq N_{1}}\{\lVert{\varrho}_{0}^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+\lVert u_{0}^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+\lVert\nabla d_{0}^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+\lVert d_{1}^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\}\leq\epsilon_{0},

where
N(a)=N_{0}-|a|h
for
0\leq|a|\leq N_{1}
. Then there exists a unique global solution
({\varrho},u,d)
of the system (
1.7
) with initial data

{\varrho}(0)={\varrho}_{0},\ \ u(0)=u_{0},\ \ d(0)=d_{0},\ \partial_{t}d(0)=d_{1},

satisfies the energy bounds

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\sup_{|a|\leq N_{1}}\{\lVert({\varrho}^{(a)},u^{(a)})(t)\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+

\displaystyle\lVert(\nabla{\varrho}^{(a)},\nabla u^{(a)})\rVert_{L^{2}([0,t]:H^{N(a)-1}\times H^{N(a)})}\}

\displaystyle+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\{\lVert\nabla d(t)\rVert_{H^{N(0)}}+\lVert\partial_{t}d(t)\rVert_{H^{N(0)}}\}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\sup_{1\leq|a|\leq N_{1}}\{\lVert\nabla d^{(a)}(t)\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{t}d^{(a)}(t)\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(1+T)^{\bar{\delta}}.

for any
T\in[0,\infty)
, where
\bar{\delta}<10^{-15}
depends on
\epsilon_{0}
,
N_{0}
and
N_{1}
.

In order to analyze the system on the Fourier side, we introduce the angles
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
. Precisely, assume that
\phi_{1}
represents the angle between
x
-axis and projection of
d
onto
x-y
plane and
\phi_{2}
represents the angle between
d
and
x-y
plane, i.e

d=(\cos\phi_{1}\cos\phi_{2},\sin\phi_{1}\cos\phi_{2},\sin\phi_{2}).

Then (
1.7
) can be rewritten as the
({\varrho},u,\phi)
-system:

(1.10)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}{\varrho}+\nabla\cdot u=F_{1},\\
&\partial_{t}u-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}\Delta u-(\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}+\mu_{2})\nabla\mathrm{div}u+a\gamma\nabla{\varrho}=F_{2},\\
&\partial_{t}^{2}\phi-\tfrac{1}{1+{\varrho}}\Delta\phi=F_{3},\end{aligned}\right.

where
\phi:=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})^{\top}
, and the nonlinear terms
F_{1},F_{2},F_{3}
are

\displaystyle F_{1}:=

\displaystyle-u\cdot\nabla{\varrho}-{\varrho}\nabla\cdot u,

\displaystyle F_{2}:=

\displaystyle-u\cdot\nabla u+(a\gamma-\tfrac{P^{\prime}(1+{\varrho})}{1+{\varrho}})\nabla{\varrho}+\tfrac{-{\varrho}}{1+{\varrho}}\big[\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}\Delta u+(\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}+\mu_{2})\nabla\mathrm{div}u\big]

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{1+{\varrho}}\big[\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla(|\nabla\phi|^{2})-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\partial_{j}(\nabla\phi\partial_{j}\phi)\big]+Err2,

\displaystyle F_{3}:=

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}u\cdot\nabla\phi-2u\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\phi-u\cdot\nabla(u\cdot\nabla\phi)+Err3,

and the error terms are

\displaystyle Err2:=-\tfrac{1}{1+{\varrho}}\big[\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla(\sin^{2}\phi_{2}|\nabla\phi_{1}|^{2})-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\partial_{j}(\sin^{2}\phi_{2}\nabla\phi_{1}\partial_{j}\phi_{1})\big],

\displaystyle Err3:=(2\tan\phi_{2}(\dot{\phi_{1}}\dot{\phi_{2}}-\tfrac{\nabla\phi_{1}\nabla\phi_{2}}{1+{\varrho}},\tfrac{1}{2}\sin 2\phi_{2}(-\dot{\phi_{1}}^{2}+\tfrac{|\nabla\phi_{1}|^{2}}{1+{\varrho}}))^{\top}.

Now it suffices to consider the system (
1.10
). For simplicity, we define

{\varrho}^{(a)}=Z^{a}{\varrho},\ u^{(a)}=Z^{a}u,\ \phi^{(a)}:=Z^{a}\phi,

Then we establish the following result:

Theorem 1.2
.

With the notation and hypothesis in Theorem
1.1
, the initial data
({\varrho}_{0},u_{0},\phi_{1,0},\phi_{1,1},\phi_{2,0},\phi_{2,1})
satisfies

(1.11)

\sup_{|a|\leq N_{1}}\{\lVert({\varrho}_{0}^{(a)},u_{0}^{(a)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\lVert\nabla\phi_{i,0}^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+\lVert\phi_{i,1}^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}})\}\leq\epsilon_{0}.

Then there exists a unique global solution
({\varrho},u,\phi)
of the system (
1.10
) with initial data

{\varrho}(0)={\varrho}_{0},\ u(0)=u_{0},\ \ \phi_{i}(0)=\phi_{i,0},\ \partial_{t}\phi_{i}(0)=\phi_{i,1},

satisfies the energy bounds

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\sup_{|a|\leq N_{1}}\{\lVert({\varrho}^{(a)},u^{(a)})(t)\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+

\displaystyle\lVert(\nabla{\varrho}^{(a)},\nabla u^{(a)})\rVert_{L^{2}([0,t]:H^{N(a)-1}\times H^{N(a)})}\}

\displaystyle+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\{\lVert\partial_{t}\phi(t)\rVert_{H^{N(0)}}+\lVert\nabla\phi(t)\rVert_{H^{N(0)}}\}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\sup_{1\leq|a|\leq N_{1}}\{\lVert\partial_{t}\phi^{(a)}(t)\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla\phi^{(a)}(t)\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(1+T)^{\bar{\delta}}.

for any
T\in[0,\infty)
, where
\bar{\delta}<10^{-15}
depends on
\epsilon_{0}
,
N_{0}
and
N_{1}
.

1.2.
Main ideas

The main strategy to prove global regularity relies on an interplay between the control of high order energies and decay estimates, which is based on the method of space-time resonances developed by Germain, Masmoudi and Shatah
[
8
,
9
,
10
]
.

Dispersive estimates and decay estimates.
In the proof the Theorem
1.2
, we need various decay estimates of
{\varrho},u
, and
\phi
. The decay of
\phi
is easily obtained by dispersive estimates of
e^{it|\nabla|}
. However, for the decay of
{\varrho}
and
u
, the linear coupling between
{\varrho}
and
u
in the first two equations in (
1.14
) brings the first difficulty: If we only use the dissipation, the decay of
\nabla{\varrho}
and
\nabla u
may be
\langle t\rangle^{-1+2\delta}
, which does not allow for the high order energy estimates of
\phi
with slow growth. Therefore, we have to investigate the wave propagation of
{\varrho}
and
u
to obtain the following faster decay rates:

(1.12)

\lVert(\nabla{\varrho},\nabla u)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-1-\delta}.

In this paper, in order to get around the first difficulty, and obtain the decay estimate (
1.12
), we introduce the “compressible part”
v
and divergence free part
w
of
u
. Precisely, let

(1.13)

v:=|\nabla|^{-1}\nabla\cdot u,\ \ w:={\mathbb{P}}u=(I+\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\cdot)u.

Conversely,
u
can be expressed in terms of
v
and
w
,

u=-|\nabla|^{-1}\nabla v+w.

Hence, we can also rewrite (
1.10
) as

(1.14)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}{\varrho}+|\nabla|v=F_{1},\\
&\partial_{t}v-(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})\Delta v-a\gamma|\nabla|{\varrho}=|\nabla|^{-1}\nabla\cdot F_{2},\\
&\partial_{t}w-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}\Delta w={\mathbb{P}}F_{2},\\
&\partial_{t}^{2}\phi-\tfrac{1}{1+{\varrho}}\Delta\phi=F_{3}.\end{aligned}\right.

From this, we observe that
w
satisfies a parabolic equation, the decay estimates of
\nabla w
are easily obtained by Duhamel’s formula and heat semi-group. Hence, it suffices to consider the decay of
\nabla{\varrho}
and
\nabla v
. For this we define the solution of linear system

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}\tilde{{\varrho}}+|\nabla|\tilde{v}=0,\\
&\partial_{t}\tilde{v}-(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})\Delta\tilde{v}-a\gamma|\nabla|\tilde{{\varrho}}=0,\\
&(\tilde{{\varrho}}(0),\tilde{v}(0))=(f,g),\end{aligned}\right.

by

(1.15)

(\tilde{{\varrho}},\tilde{v})^{\top}=e^{tB}(f,g)^{\top},

where the linear operator
e^{tB}
defined as

(1.16)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}(e^{tB}(f,g)^{\top})(\xi)=

\displaystyle e^{tB(\xi)}(\widehat{f},\widehat{g})^{\top}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} &\tfrac{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)e^{\Lambda_{-}(\xi)t}-\Lambda_{-}e^{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)t}}{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)-\Lambda_{-}(\xi)}&|\xi|\tfrac{e^{\Lambda_{-}(\xi)t}-e^{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)t}}{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)-\Lambda_{-}(\xi)}\\
&a\gamma|\xi|\tfrac{e^{\Lambda_{-}(\xi)t}-e^{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)t}}{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)-\Lambda_{-}(\xi)}&\tfrac{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)e^{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)t}-\Lambda_{-}(\xi)e^{\Lambda_{-}(\xi)t}}{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)-\Lambda_{-}(\xi)}\end{aligned}\right)\left(\begin{aligned} \widehat{f}(\xi)\\
\widehat{g}(\xi)\end{aligned}\right),

and

(1.17)

\Lambda_{\pm}(\xi):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &-\tfrac{\mu}{2}|\xi|^{2}\pm i\sqrt{a\gamma|\xi|^{2}-\tfrac{\mu^{2}}{4}|\xi|^{4}},\ \ \ \ \ \ |\xi|\leq\tfrac{2\sqrt{a\gamma}}{\mu},\ \mu=\mu_{1}+\mu_{2},\\
&-\tfrac{\mu}{2}|\xi|^{2}\pm\sqrt{\tfrac{\mu^{2}}{4}|\xi|^{4}-a\gamma|\xi|^{2}},\ \ \ \ \ \ \ |\xi|>\tfrac{2\sqrt{a\gamma}}{\mu}.\end{aligned}\right.

We divide the above linear solution into the low-frequency case and high-frequency case, i.e

(1.18)

e^{tB}(f,g)^{\top}=P_{\leq k}e^{tB}(f,g)^{\top}+P_{>k}e^{tB}(f,g)^{\top},

Then by Fourier analysis, we may have the following linear estimates:

(1.19)

\lVert e^{tB}\nabla^{l}(f,g)^{\top}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{3}{2p}-\frac{l}{2}}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{L^{p}}+e^{-ct}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{H^{l+2}},\ 1\leq p\leq 2.

For low-frequency term,

(1.20)

\lVert P_{\leq k}e^{tB}\nabla(f,g)^{\top}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-5/4}2^{k}\lVert|\nabla|^{-1}(f,g)\rVert_{L^{2}},

and for high-frequency term,

(1.21)

\lVert P_{>k}e^{tB}\nabla(f,g)^{\top}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3/2}2^{-13k^{-}}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}.

The decomposition (
1.18
) and a similar linear estimates (
1.21
) when
k=0
were investigated by Li and Zhang
[
30
]
. Here when we choose
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
satisfying
2^{k}\approx\langle t\rangle^{-c}
for sufficient small
c>0
, the decay estimate (
1.12
) can be obtained by Duhamel’s formula, (
1.19
), (
1.20
) and (
1.21
).

Energy estimates.
We would like to control the increment of both high order Sobolev norms and weighted norms for solutions of (
1.10
). In fact, by the dissipation of
{\varrho}
and
u
and the decay estimates, we can obtain that the energies of
{\varrho}^{(a)},u^{(a)}
for
0\leq|a|\leq N_{1}
, and
\phi
are bounded. However, for the energy estimates of
\phi^{(a)}
,
1\leq|a|\leq N_{1}
, the presence of time resonances without matching null structure brings a second difficulty. More precisely, in proving the energy estimates, the main difficulty are caused by the quadratic terms
{\varrho}^{(b)}\Delta\phi^{(c)}
and
u^{(b)}\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\phi^{(c)}
.

To get around the main difficulty, we discuss the term
{\varrho}^{(b)}\Delta\phi^{(c)}
in detail. Let

\Phi:=\partial_{t}\phi+i|\nabla|\phi,\ \ \Phi^{(a)}:=\partial_{t}\phi^{(a)}+i|\nabla|\phi^{(a)}.

In order to control the increment of
\phi^{(a)}
for
1\leq|a|\leq N_{1}
, by
\phi
-equation one needs to consider the space-time integrals

(1.22)

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}m(\xi,\eta)\overline{\widehat{P_{k}\Phi^{(a)}}}(\xi)\widehat{P_{k_{1}}{\varrho}^{(b)}}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{P_{k_{2}}\Phi^{(c)}}(\eta)d\xi d\eta ds,

for any
t\in[0,T]
,
k,k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, and
m(\xi,\eta):=(1+\xi^{2N(a)})|\eta|
. Notice that it is not possible to estimate (
1.22
) by moving the absolute value inside the time integral, due to the slow decay of
u^{(b)}
in
L^{\infty}
. So we need to integrate by parts in time or
\eta
, for this define the profiles

\Psi:=e^{-it|\nabla|}\Phi,\ \ \Psi^{(a)}:=e^{-it|\nabla|}\Phi^{(a)}.

Then after rewriting the functions
\Phi^{(a)}
,
\Phi^{(c)}
in terms of the variables
\Psi^{(a)}
,
\Psi^{(c)}
, we need to consider the space-time integral

(1.23)

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}e^{-is(|\xi|-|\eta|)}m(\xi,\eta)\overline{\widehat{P_{k}\Psi^{(a)}}}(\xi)\widehat{P_{k_{1}}{\varrho}^{(b)}}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{P_{k_{2}}\Psi^{(c)}}(\eta)d\xi d\eta ds,

We observe that the above integral admits the time resonant frequencies

\mathscr{T}=\{(\xi,\eta):|\xi|-|\eta|=0,m(\xi,\eta)\neq 0\},

but the set of space resonances is null, i.e

\mathcal{S}=\{(\xi,\eta):\nabla_{\eta}(|\xi|-|\eta|)=0,m(\xi,\eta)\neq 0\}=\emptyset.

Hence, for the contribution of high-low interaction, i.e
k_{1}\sim k,k_{2}<k-5
and high-high interaction, i.e
k_{1}\sim k_{2}>k+5
, we may integrate by parts in time and then use the decay of
\partial_{s}{\varrho}^{(b)}
and
\partial_{s}\Psi^{(c)}
to control (
1.23
). For the contribution of low-high interaction, i.e
k\sim k_{2},k_{1}\leq k+6
, we may use integration by parts in
\eta
and the decay of
\lVert\nabla{\varrho}^{(b)}\rVert_{L^{2}}
and
\Phi^{(c)}
to control the increment of (
1.23
).

L^{2}
-bounds on the derivatives of
\widehat{\Psi^{(a)}}
.
In the above arguments, we also need the following bootstrap assumptions

\lVert{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}(|\xi|\partial_{\xi}\widehat{\Psi^{(a)}})\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{H(a+1)\delta},\ \ |a|\leq N_{1}-1,

which corresponds to weighted bounds in the physical space and can be linked to the energy estimates by

\lVert{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}(|\xi|\partial_{\xi}\widehat{\Psi^{(a)}})\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\lesssim\lVert S\Psi^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+\lVert\Omega\Psi^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+\lVert\Psi^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+\lVert t\partial_{t}\Psi^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}.

1.3.
Notations

For
N\geq 0
,
1\leq p\leq\infty
, let
L^{p},H^{N},\dot{H}^{N},W^{N,p}
and
\dot{W}^{N,p}
denote the standard Sobolev spaces on
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, i.e

L^{p}:=L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}),\ H^{N}:=H^{N}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}),\ \dot{H}^{N}:=\dot{H}^{N}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}),\ W^{N,p}:=W^{N,p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}),\ \dot{W}^{N,p}:=\dot{W}^{N,p}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}).

For any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, let
k^{+}:=\max(k,0)
and
k^{-}:=\min(k,0)
. For any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
, denote
\langle x\rangle:=\sqrt{1+x^{2}}
. For any two numbers
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
and a absolute constant
C
, we denote

M_{1}\lesssim M_{2},\ M_{2}\gtrsim M_{1},\ \mathrm{if}\ M_{1}\leq CM_{2}.

Here we will use the following multi-index

n:=(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}.

Denote

|n|:=\alpha+\beta+\gamma,\ \ \partial^{n}:=\partial^{\alpha}_{x_{1}}\partial^{\beta}_{x_{2}}\partial^{\gamma}_{x_{3}}.

1.4.
Outline

In section 2, we fix notations and state the main bootstrap proposition
2.8
. We also state several lemmas, such as dispersive linear bounds, Hardy-type estimate and weighted
L^{\infty}-L^{2}
estimate. In section 3, we use the bootstrap assumptions to prove various decay estimates of
{\varrho},u
and
\phi
. Then we start the proof of the main bootstrap proposition
2.8
in section 4, 5, and 6, where we obtain improved energy estimates (
2.23
) and the
L^{2}
bounds (
2.24
) on the derivation of
\widehat{\Psi}
.

2.
Preliminaries and the main proposition

In this section, we start by summarizing our main definitions and notations.

2.1.
Some analysis tools

The Fourier transform of
f
is defined as follows:

\mathcal{F}(f)(\xi):=\widehat{f}(\xi)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{-ix\cdot\xi}f(x)dx.

We use
\mathcal{F}^{-1}(f)
to denote the inverse Fourier transform of
f
. Fix an even smooth function
\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow[0,1]
supported in
[-2,2]
and equal to 1 in
[-1,1]
. For simplicity of notation, we also let
\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\rightarrow[0,1]
denote the corresponding radial function on
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
. For any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
, let

\displaystyle\varphi_{k}(x):=\varphi(x/2^{k})-\varphi(x/2^{k-1}),\ \ \varphi_{I}(x):=\sum_{m\in I\cap{\mathbb{Z}}}\varphi_{m}(x),

\displaystyle\varphi_{\leq k}(x):=\sum_{l\leq k}\varphi_{l}(x),\ \varphi_{>k}(x):=\sum_{l>k}\varphi_{l}(x).

The frequency projection operator
P_{k}
,
P_{I}
,
P_{\leq k}
and
P_{>k}
is defined by the Fourier multiplier
\varphi_{k}(\xi)
,
\varphi_{I}(\xi)
,
\varphi_{\leq k}(\xi)
and
\varphi_{>k}(\xi)
, i.e.

\displaystyle\widehat{P_{k}f}(\xi)=\varphi_{k}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi),\ \ \ \widehat{P_{I}f}(\xi)=\varphi_{I}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi),

\displaystyle\widehat{P_{\leq k}f}(\xi)=\varphi_{\leq k}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)\ \mathrm{and}\ \widehat{P_{>k}f}(\xi)=\varphi_{>k}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi).

Moreover, we have the following Bernstein inequality: For any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

(2.1)

\lVert P_{k}f\rVert_{L^{q}}\lesssim 2^{3k(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})}\lVert P_{k}f\rVert_{L^{p}},\ \ \ 1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty.

Let

\mathcal{J}:=\{(k,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{+}:k+j\geq 0\}.

For any
(k,j)\in\mathcal{J}
, let

\tilde{\varphi}_{j}^{(k)}(x):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\varphi_{\leq-k}(x),\ \mathrm{if}\ k+j=0\ \mathrm{and}\ k\leq 0,\\
&\varphi_{\leq 0}(x),\ \mathrm{if}\ j=0\ \mathrm{and}\ k\geq 0,\\
&\varphi_{j}(x),\ \mathrm{if}\ k+j\geq 1\ \mathrm{and}\ j\geq 1,\end{aligned}\right.

then for any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
fixed,
\sum_{j\geq\max(-k,0)}\tilde{\varphi}_{j}^{(k)}=1
. For any
(k,j)\in\mathcal{J}
, we define the operator
Q_{jk}
by

(Q_{jk}f)(x):=\tilde{\varphi}_{j}^{(k)}(x)\cdot P_{k}f(x),

and denote

f_{j,k}(x):=P_{[k-2,k+2]}Q_{jk}f(x).

Then
P_{k}f
for fix
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
can be decomposed as

(2.2)

P_{k}f(x)=P_{[k-2,k+2]}P_{k}f(x)=P_{[k-2,k+2]}\sum_{j\geq\max(-k,0)}\tilde{\varphi}_{j}^{(k)}(x)P_{k}f(x)=\sum_{j\geq\max(-k,0)}f_{j,k}(x).

Moreover, for any
f\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
and
(k,j)\in\mathcal{J}
, let
\beta:=1/1000
, we have

(2.3)

\lVert\widehat{f_{j,k}}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\min\{2^{3j/2}\lVert Q_{jk}f\rVert_{L^{2}},\ 2^{j/2-k}2^{\beta(j+k)}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 1}\lVert Q_{jk}\Omega^{\alpha}f\rVert_{L^{2}}\},

see
[
14
, Lemma 3.4.]
.

We state several decay estimates for some linear operators.

Lemma 2.1

(Linear decay estimates)
.

(i) For any
f\in\mathcal{S}
and
l\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we have

(2.4)

\lVert e^{t\Delta}|\nabla|^{l}f\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{3}{2p}-\frac{l}{2}}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{p}},\ \ \ \ 1\leq p\leq\infty.

(ii) For any
f,g\in\mathcal{S}
and
l\in{\mathbb{N}}
, for the operator (
1.15
) we have

(2.5)

\displaystyle\lVert e^{tB}|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)^{\top}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{3}{2p}-\frac{l}{2}}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{L^{p}}+e^{-ct}\lVert|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{H^{2}},\ \ 1\leq p\leq 2,

(2.6)

\displaystyle\lVert e^{tB}|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)^{\top}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{l}{2}}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{L^{2}}+e^{-ct}\lVert|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{L^{2}},

(2.7)

\displaystyle\lVert P_{>k_{0}}e^{tB}(f,g)^{\top}\rVert_{L^{p}}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3/2}2^{-13k_{0}^{-}}\lVert P_{>k_{0}}(f,g)\rVert_{L^{p}},\ \ {\rm for}\ 2\leq p\leq\infty,k_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

Proof.

The proof of (
2.4
) is standard. Here we prove the bound (
2.5
)-(
2.7
) in detail.

Step 1. We prove the bound (
2.5
).
From the definition of
\Lambda_{\pm}
(
1.17
), we easily check that

(2.8)

|e^{tB(\xi)}|\lesssim\left\{\begin{aligned} &e^{-c|\xi|^{2}t},\ \ \ \text{for }|\xi|\leq\tfrac{2\sqrt{a\gamma}}{\mu},\\
&e^{-ct},\ \ \ \text{for }|\xi|>\tfrac{2\sqrt{a\gamma}}{\mu},\end{aligned}\right.

where
c>0
small. Then for
t\leq 1
, using Sobolev embedding it follows that

\lVert e^{tB}|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\lVert e^{tB}|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{H^{2}}\lesssim\lVert|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{H^{2}}.

For
t>1
, let
j_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
satisfy
2^{j_{0}}\approx\frac{2\sqrt{a\gamma}}{\mu}
, from Young’s inequality, Sobolev embedding, Hausdorff-Young inequality and (
2.8
) we obtain for
1\leq p\leq 2
and
q^{-1}+p^{-1}=1

\displaystyle\lVert e^{tB}|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{\leq j_{0}}(|\nabla|)e^{tB}|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\lVert\varphi_{>j_{0}}(|\nabla|)e^{tB}|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}(\varphi_{\leq j_{0}}(|\xi|)e^{tB(\xi)}|\xi|^{l})\rVert_{L^{q}}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{L^{p}}+\lVert\varphi_{>j_{0}}(|\nabla|)e^{tB}\langle\nabla\rangle^{2}|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert e^{-c|\xi|^{2}t}|\xi|^{l}\rVert_{L^{p}}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{L^{p}}+\lVert\varphi_{>j_{0}}(|\xi|)e^{tB(\xi)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\langle\nabla\rangle^{2}|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{-\frac{3}{2p}-\frac{l}{2}}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{L^{p}}+e^{-ct}\lVert\langle\nabla\rangle^{2}|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{L^{2}}.

This concludes the proof of (
2.5
).

Step 2. We prove the bound (
2.6
).

By (
2.8
) and Plancherel’s Theorem we get this bound

\displaystyle\lVert e^{tB}|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)^{\top}\rVert_{L^{2}}=

\displaystyle\lVert e^{tB(\xi)}|\xi|^{l}(\widehat{f}(\xi),\widehat{g}(\xi)^{\top}\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{\leq j_{0}}(\xi)e^{-c|\xi|^{2}t}|\xi|^{l}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\varphi_{>j_{0}}(\xi)e^{-ct}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{-l/2}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{L^{2}}+e^{-ct}\lVert|\nabla|^{l}(f,g)\rVert_{L^{2}}.

Step 3. We prove the bound (
2.7
).

The following bound

\lVert P_{>j_{0}}e^{tB}(f,g)^{\top}\rVert_{L^{p}}\lesssim e^{-ct}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{L^{p}},

for some
c>0
small is given in
[
30
, Lemma 4.3]
. Then it suffices to show that

\lVert P_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}e^{tB}(f,g)^{\top}\rVert_{L^{p}}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3/2}2^{-13k_{0}}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{L^{p}}.

By Young’s inequality we have

\displaystyle\lVert P_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}e^{tB}(f,g)^{\top}\rVert_{L^{p}}=

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}e^{tB(\xi)})\ast(f,g)^{\top}\rVert_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}e^{tB(\xi)})\rVert_{L^{1}}\lVert(f,g)\rVert_{L^{p}}.

Now it suffices to show the bound

\lVert\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}e^{tB(\xi)})\rVert_{L^{1}}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3/2}2^{-13k_{0}}.

By integration by parts in frequency, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}e^{tB(\xi)})\rVert_{L^{1}}=

\displaystyle\lVert(\varphi_{<0}(x)+\varphi_{\geq 0}(x))\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}e^{tB(\xi)})\rVert_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert|x|^{-2}\varphi_{<0}(x)\rVert_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\lVert\nabla_{\xi}^{2}(\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}(\xi)e^{tB(\xi)})\rVert_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle+\lVert|x|^{-4}\varphi_{\geq 0}(x)\rVert_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\lVert\nabla_{\xi}^{4}(\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}(\xi)e^{tB(\xi)})\rVert_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla_{\xi}^{2}(\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}(\xi)e^{tB(\xi)})\rVert_{L^{1}}+\lVert\nabla_{\xi}^{4}(\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}(\xi)e^{tB(\xi)})\rVert_{L^{1}}.

From the definition of
e^{tB(\xi)}
(
1.16
), we have

\displaystyle m(\xi):=\frac{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)e^{\Lambda_{-}(\xi)t}-\Lambda_{-}(\xi)e^{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)t}}{\Lambda_{+}(\xi)-\Lambda_{-}(\xi)}=\big(\frac{\mu}{2}|\xi|^{2}t\frac{\sin bt}{bt}+\cos bt\big)e^{-\frac{\mu}{2}|\xi|^{2}t},

where

b:=\sqrt{a\gamma|\xi|^{2}-\frac{\mu^{2}}{4}|\xi|^{4}}.

With the restriction
\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}(\xi)
, by direct computations we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\xi}\big(\frac{\sin bt}{bt}\big)\lesssim|\xi|t^{2}\lesssim(|\xi|^{2}t)^{2}2^{-3k_{0}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\xi}^{2}\big(\frac{\sin bt}{bt}\big)\lesssim|\xi|^{2}t^{4}+t^{2}\lesssim(1+|\xi|^{2}t)^{4}2^{-6k_{0}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\xi}^{3}\big(\frac{\sin bt}{bt}\big)\lesssim|\xi|^{3}t^{6}+|\xi|t^{4}+t^{2}\lesssim(1+|\xi|^{2}t)^{6}2^{-9k_{0}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\xi}^{4}\big(\frac{\sin bt}{bt}\big)\lesssim|\xi|^{4}t^{8}+|\xi|^{2}t^{6}+t^{4}+t^{2}\lesssim(1+|\xi|^{2}t)^{8}2^{-12k_{0}}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\xi}^{l}(\cos bt)\lesssim(1+|\xi|^{2}t)^{2l}2^{-3lk_{0}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\xi}^{l}e^{-\frac{\mu}{2}|\xi|^{2}t}\lesssim(1+|\xi|^{2}t)^{l}2^{-lk_{0}}e^{-\frac{\mu}{2}|\xi|^{2}t}.

From these estimates, we

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla_{\xi}^{2}(\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}(\xi)m(\xi))\rVert_{L^{1}}+\lVert\nabla_{\xi}^{4}(\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}(\xi)m(\xi))\rVert_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\varphi_{[k_{0},j_{0}]}(\xi)(1+|\xi|^{2}t)^{9}2^{-13k_{0}}e^{-\frac{\mu}{2}|\xi|^{2}t}d\xi\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3/2}2^{-13k_{0}}.

For other symbols in
e^{tB(\xi)}
, we have the same estimates. This completes the proof of (
2.7
).

∎

We also need the following Hardy-type estimate involving localization in frequency and space.

Lemma 2.2

(Lemma 3.5,
[
14
]
)
.

For
f\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
and
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
let

F_{k}(f):=\lVert P_{k}f\rVert_{L^{2}}+\sum\limits_{l=1}^{3}\lVert\varphi_{k}(\xi)\partial_{\xi_{l}}\widehat{f}(\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}},\ \ B_{k}(f):=\big[\sum\limits_{j\geq\max(-k,0)}2^{2j}\lVert Q_{jk}f\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\big]^{1/2}.

Then, for any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

B_{k}\lesssim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{|k^{\prime}-k|\leq 4}F_{k^{\prime}},\ \mathrm{if}\ k\geq 0,\\
&\sum_{k^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}F_{k^{\prime}}2^{-|k-k^{\prime}|/2}\min\{1,2^{k^{\prime}-k}\},\ \mathrm{if}\ k\leq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

Lemma 2.3

(Lemma 2.2,
[
33
]
)
.

If
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{6}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
and
k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, then the following estimate holds:

\lVert\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{6}}f(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})e^{i(x_{1}\cdot\xi_{1}+x_{2}\cdot\xi_{2})}\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi_{1})\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi_{2})d\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\rVert_{L^{1}_{x_{1},x_{2}}}\lesssim\sum\limits_{m=0}^{7}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{2}2^{mk_{j}}\lVert\partial_{\xi_{j}}^{m}f\rVert_{L^{\infty}}.

To obtain the energy estimates, one often needs to analyze the symbols. Define a class of symbol as follows

\mathcal{S}^{\infty}:=\{m:{\mathbb{R}}^{6}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}},\ m\ \text{is continous and }\lVert\mathcal{F}^{-1}m\rVert_{L^{1}}<\infty\},

whose associated norms are defined as

\lVert m\rVert_{\mathcal{S}^{\infty}}:=\lVert\mathcal{F}^{-1}m\rVert_{L^{1}},

and

\lVert m\rVert_{\mathcal{S}^{\infty}_{k,k_{1},k_{2}}}:=\lVert m(\xi,\eta)\varphi_{k}(\xi)\varphi_{k_{1}}(\xi-\eta)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\eta)\rVert_{\mathcal{S^{\infty}}}.

Then we have

Lemma 2.4

(Bilinear estimate,
[
15
]
)
.

Given
m\in\mathcal{S}^{\infty}
and two well-defined functions
f_{1},\ f_{2}
, then the following estimate holds:

(2.9)

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}m(\xi,\eta)\widehat{f}_{1}(\xi-\eta)\widehat{f}_{2}(\eta)d\eta\big)(x)\rVert_{L^{r}}\lesssim\lVert m\rVert_{\mathcal{S}^{\infty}}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{L^{p}}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{L^{q}},\ \ \frac{1}{r}=\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}.

Lemma 2.5

(Hardy-type inequality and interpolation inequality)
.

Given
1\leq p\leq\infty
,
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and two well-defined functions
f
,
g
, then the following estimates holds:

(2.10)

\displaystyle\lVert fg\rVert_{\dot{H}^{N}}\lesssim\sum_{k}2^{Nk^{+}}\lVert P_{k}f\rVert_{\infty}\lVert g\rVert_{\dot{H}^{N}},

(2.11)

\displaystyle\lVert fg\rVert_{\dot{H}^{N}}\lesssim\lVert f\rVert_{\dot{H}^{N}}\lVert g\rVert_{\infty}+\lVert f\rVert_{\infty}\lVert g\rVert_{\dot{H}^{N}},

(2.12)

\displaystyle\lVert f\rVert_{L^{p}}\lesssim\lVert f\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{1-2/p}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2/p},\ {\rm for}\ 2\leq p\leq\infty.

Proof.

The proof of the first two bounds is standard by Littlewood-Paley decomposition, see Lemma 2.5 in
[
13
]
for detail. The last bound is obtained by Hölder immediately.
∎

Finally, we need the following weighted
L^{\infty}-L^{2}
estimate and weighted
H^{N}
estiamtes.

Lemma 2.6

(Lemma 3.3,
[
31
]
)
.

Let
f\in H^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
,
r=|x|
, then there holds

(2.13)

\lVert\langle r\rangle f\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 1}\lVert\partial_{r}\tilde{\Omega}^{\alpha}f\rVert_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\lVert\tilde{\Omega}^{\alpha}f\rVert_{L^{2}}^{1/2},

provided the right hand side is finite.

Lemma 2.7
.

For any
N\geq 0
, we have

(2.14)

\lVert{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}(|\xi|\nabla_{\xi}\widehat{f})\rVert_{H^{N}}\lesssim\lVert x\cdot\nabla_{x}f\rVert_{H^{N}}+\lVert\Omega f\rVert_{H^{N}}+\lVert f\rVert_{H^{N}}.

Proof.

This bound is obtained by the relation

|\xi|\nabla_{\xi}=\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}(\xi\cdot\nabla_{\xi})-\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\wedge\Omega(\xi),

where
\Omega(\xi)=\xi\wedge\nabla_{\xi}
.
∎

2.2.
The main bootstrap proposition

By the similar argument to
[
1
, Section 2.1]
, applying the vector fields
Z
to the system (
1.10
), we obtain

(2.15)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}Z^{a}{\varrho}+\nabla\cdot Z^{a}u=F_{1}^{(a)},\\
&\partial_{t}Z^{a}u-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}\Delta(S-1)^{a_{1}}\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}u-(\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}+\mu_{2})\nabla\mathrm{div}(S-1)^{a_{1}}\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}u+\tfrac{P^{\prime}(1+{\varrho})}{1+{\varrho}}\nabla Z^{a}{\varrho}=F_{2}^{(a)},\\
&\partial_{t}^{2}Z^{a}\phi-\tfrac{1}{1+{\varrho}}\Delta Z^{a}\phi=F_{3}^{(a)},\end{aligned}\right.

where the nonlinear terms are

(2.16)

F_{1}^{(a)}:=-\sum_{b+c=a}C^{b}_{a}(Z^{b}u\cdot\nabla Z^{c}{\varrho}+Z^{b}{\varrho}\cdot\nabla Z^{c}u),

(2.17)

\displaystyle F_{2}^{(a)}:=

\displaystyle-\sum_{b+c=a}C^{b}_{a}Z^{b}u\cdot\nabla Z^{c}u-\sum_{b+c=a,|b|\geq 1}C^{b}_{a}Z^{b}\Big(\tfrac{P^{\prime}(1+{\varrho})}{1+{\varrho}}\Big)\nabla Z^{c}{\varrho}

\displaystyle-\sum_{b+c=a}C^{b}_{a}Z^{b}\Big(\tfrac{{\varrho}}{1+{\varrho}}\Big)\Big[\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}\Delta(S-1)^{(c_{1})}\Gamma^{(c^{\prime})}u+(\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}+\mu_{2})\nabla\mathrm{div}(S-1)^{c_{1}}\Gamma^{c^{\prime}}u\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{b+c+e=a}C^{b,c}_{a}Z^{b}\Big(\tfrac{1}{1+{\varrho}}\Big)\Big[\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla\big(\nabla(S-1)^{c_{1}}\Gamma^{c^{\prime}}\phi\cdot\nabla(S-1)^{e_{1}}\Gamma^{e^{\prime}}\phi\big)

\displaystyle-\partial_{j}\big(\nabla(S-1)^{c_{1}}\Gamma^{c^{\prime}}\phi\cdot\partial_{j}(S-1)^{e_{1}}\Gamma^{e^{\prime}}\phi\big)\Big]+(S+1)^{a_{1}}\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}Err2,

(2.18)

\displaystyle F^{(a)}_{3}:=

\displaystyle-\sum_{b+c=a,|b|\geq 1}C^{b}_{a}Z^{b}\Big(\tfrac{1}{1+{\varrho}}\Big)\Delta Z^{c}\phi-\sum_{b+c=a}C_{a}^{b}(\partial_{t}Z^{b}u\cdot\nabla Z^{c}\phi+2Z^{b}u\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}Z^{c}\phi)

\displaystyle-\sum_{b+c+e=a}C_{a}^{b,c}Z^{b}u\cdot\nabla(Z^{c}u\cdot\nabla Z^{e}\phi)+(S+2)^{a_{1}}\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}Err3.

and the constants are

C_{a}^{b}:=\tfrac{a!}{b!(a-b)!},\ C_{a}^{b,c}:=\tfrac{a!}{b!c!(a-b-c)!}.

In order to prove the decay estimates of
\nabla{\varrho}^{(a)}
and
\nabla u^{(a)}
, we define

v^{(a)}:=|\nabla|^{-1}\nabla\cdot u^{(a)}=|\nabla|^{-1}\nabla\cdot Z^{a}u,\ w^{(a)}:={\mathbb{P}}u^{(a)}={\mathbb{P}}Z^{a}u.

Then the system (
2.15
) can be rewritten as

(2.19)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}{\varrho}^{(a)}+|\nabla|v^{(a)}=F_{1}^{(a)},\\
&\partial_{t}v^{(a)}-(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})\Delta v^{(a)}-a\gamma|\nabla|{\varrho}^{(a)}=|\nabla|^{-1}\nabla\cdot\tilde{F}^{(a)}_{2},\\
&\partial_{t}w^{(a)}-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}\Delta w^{(a)}={\mathbb{P}}\tilde{F}_{2}^{(a)},\\
&\partial_{t}^{2}\phi^{(a)}-\tfrac{1}{1+{\varrho}}\Delta\phi^{(a)}=F_{3}^{(a)},\end{aligned}\right.

where the nonlinear term
\tilde{F}_{2}^{(a)}
is

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{2}^{(a)}:=\sum_{l=1}^{a_{1}}C_{a_{1}}^{l}(-1)^{l}\big(\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}\Delta S^{a_{1}-l}\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}u+(\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}+\mu_{2})\nabla\mathrm{div}S^{a_{1}-l}\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}u\big)+(a\gamma-\tfrac{P^{\prime}(1+{\varrho})}{1+{\varrho}})\nabla{\varrho}^{(a)}+F_{2}^{(a)}.

To state the main proposition we define the normalized solution
\Phi
and its profile
\Psi
by

\displaystyle\Phi(t):=(\partial_{t}+i|\nabla|)\phi(t),\ \ \Psi(t):=e^{-it|\nabla|}\Phi(t),

which will play an important role in the energy estimates. The function
\phi
can be recovered from the normalized variable
\Phi
by the formulas

\partial_{t}\phi=(\Phi+\overline{\Phi})/2,\ \ |\nabla|\phi=(\Phi-\overline{\Phi})/2i.

More generally, we define
\Phi^{(a)}
and
\Psi^{(a)}
for
1\leq|a|\leq N_{1}
by

(2.20)

\displaystyle\Phi^{(a)}(t):=(\partial_{t}+i|\nabla|)\phi^{(a)}(t),\ \ \Psi^{(a)}(t):=e^{-it|\nabla|}\Phi^{(a)}(t).

Theorem
1.2
is a consequence of the following proposition:

Proposition 2.8
.

Assume that
({\varrho},u,\phi)
is a solution to (
1.10
) on some time interval
[0,T]
,
T\geq 1
with initial data satisfying the assumptions (
1.11
). Assume also that, for any
t\in[0,T]
, the solution satisfies the bootstrap hypothesis

(2.21)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{|a|\leq N_{1},t\in[0,T]}\{\lVert({\varrho}^{(a)},u^{(a)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+\lVert(\nabla{\varrho}^{(a)},\nabla u^{(a)})\rVert_{L^{2}([0,t]:H^{N(a)-1}\times H^{N(a)})}+\langle t\rangle^{-H(a)\delta}\lVert\Phi^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\}\leq\epsilon_{1},

(2.22)

\displaystyle\sup_{|a|\leq N_{1}-1,t\in[0,T]}\langle t\rangle^{-H(|a|+1)\delta}\lVert\mathcal{F}^{-1}(|\xi|\partial_{\xi}\widehat{\Psi^{(a)}})\rVert_{H^{N(|a|+1)}}\leq\epsilon_{1},

where
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{0}^{2/3}
,
\delta=10^{-15}
,

\displaystyle H(0)=0;\ H(a)=1,\ \mathrm{for}\ 1\leq|a|\leq N_{1}-2;\ H(a)=2,\ \mathrm{for}\ N_{1}-1\leq|a|\leq N_{1}.

\displaystyle N(a)=N_{0}-|a|h,\ N_{0}=60,\ h=6.

Then the following improved bounds hold, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

(2.23)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{|a|\leq N_{1},t\in[0,T]}\{\lVert({\varrho}^{(a)},v^{(a)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+\lVert(\nabla{\varrho}^{(a)},\nabla v^{(a)})\rVert_{L^{2}([0,t]:H^{N(a)-1}\times H^{N(a)})}+\langle t\rangle^{-H(a)\delta}\lVert\Phi^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

(2.24)

\displaystyle\sup_{|a|\leq N_{1}-1,t\in[0,T]}\langle t\rangle^{-H(|a|+1)\delta}\lVert\mathcal{F}^{-1}(|\xi|\partial_{\xi}\widehat{\Psi^{(a)}})\rVert_{H^{N(|a|+1)}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

The bounds (
2.22
) and (
2.24
) are our main
L^{2}
bounds on the derivatives of the profile
\Psi^{(a)}
in the Fourier space. They correspond to weighted bounds in the physical space which play an important role in the energy estimates.

Before we proceed, as in
[
13
, Corollary 2.8]
, Lemma
2.2
and the bootstrap assumptions of Proposition
2.8
yield

Corollary 2.9
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
2.8
, we have

(2.25)

\Big(\sum_{(k,j)\in\mathcal{J}}2^{2N(|a|+1)k^{+}+2k}2^{2j}\lVert Q_{jk}\Psi^{(a)}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{H(|a|+1)\delta},

for any
|a|\leq N_{1}-1
.

Once Proposition
2.8
is proved, Theorem
1.2
follows directly from the standard continuity argument. The rest of this paper focuses on the proof of Proposition
2.8
. The key ingredients include Proposition
\ref{Prop_Ene_Sob}-\ref{Prop_Ene_Weiphi}
and Proposition
6.1
.

3.
Decay of density, velocity field and angles

In this section, we give the various decay estimates of
{\varrho}
,
u
and
\Phi
, which will be useful in the energy estimates in the next sections.

3.1.
Decay of
\phi

In order for the decay estimates of
\phi
, the following frequency localized linear dispersive estimate is necessary.

Lemma 3.1

(Lemma 3.1,
[
13
]
)
.

Frequency localized linear decay estimate] For any
t\in[1,T]
and
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have

(3.1)

\lVert e^{it|\nabla|}P_{k}f\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\lVert\widehat{P_{k}f}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}(t^{-1}2^{2k}+t^{-1+\delta}2^{2k+\delta k})+\lVert\nabla_{\xi}\widehat{P_{k}f}\rVert_{L^{2}}(t^{-1}2^{3k/2}+t^{-1+\delta}2^{3k/2+\delta k}).

Lemma 3.2
.

Under the bootstrap assumption (
2.22
), for any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
t\in[0,T]
and
|a|\leq N_{1}
we have

(3.2)

\displaystyle\lVert P_{k}\Phi^{(a)}(t)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta+H(|a|+2)\delta}2^{k^{-}/2}2^{-N(|a|+1)k^{+}+(h/2+2)k^{+}},

(3.3)

\displaystyle\lVert|\nabla|^{-1}\Phi^{(a)}(t)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-1/2+3\delta}.

Proof.

On one hand, by (
2.3
) and (
2.25
) when
k>0
, we have

(3.4)

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{P_{k}\Psi^{(a)}}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j\leq hk^{+}}2^{3j/2}\lVert Q_{jk}\Psi^{(a)}\rVert_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j>hk^{+}}2^{j/2-k+\beta(j+k)}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 1}\lVert Q_{jk}\tilde{\Omega}^{\alpha}\Psi^{(a)}\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{hk^{+}/2}\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{H(|a|+2)\delta}2^{-N(|a|+1)k^{+}-k}+2^{-hk^{+}/2}\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{H(|a|+2)\delta}2^{-N(|a|+2)k^{+}-k}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{H(|a|+2)\delta}2^{-N(|a|+1)k^{+}+hk^{+}/2-k}.

On the other hand, when
k\leq 0
we have

(3.5)

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{P_{k}\Psi^{(a)}}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq-k}2^{j/2-k+\beta(j+k)}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 1}\lVert Q_{jk}\tilde{\Omega}^{\alpha}\Psi^{(a)}\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{H(|a|+2)\delta}2^{-3k/2}.

Meanwhile, by (
2.22
) and the definition of
P_{k}
, we also have

(3.6)

2^{k}\lVert\nabla_{\xi}\widehat{P_{k}\Psi^{(a)}}(\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}}=\lVert\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\varphi_{k}^{\prime}(\xi)\widehat{\Psi^{(a)}}(\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}}+2^{k}\lVert\varphi_{k}(\xi)\nabla_{\xi}\widehat{\Psi^{(a)}}\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{H(|a|+1)\delta}2^{-N(|a|+1)k^{+}}.

Thus (
3.2
) follows from (
3.4
), (
3.5
), (
3.6
) and (
3.1
).

Next by (
2.1
), Hölder inequality, (
2.21
) and (
3.2
), we have for
p\geq 2

\displaystyle\lVert|\nabla|^{-1}\Phi^{(a)}(t)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k}2^{3k/p-k}\lVert P_{k}\Phi^{(a)}(t)\rVert_{L^{p}}\lesssim\sum_{k}2^{3k/p-k}\lVert P_{k}\Phi^{(a)}(t)\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2/p}\lVert P_{k}\Phi^{(a)}(t)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}^{1-2/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{1}t^{(-1+\delta+H(|a|+2)\delta)(1-2/p)}\sum_{k}2^{(2/p-1/2)k^{-}}2^{(1-2/p)(-N(|a|+2)+h/2+2)k^{+}+k^{+}/p}.

Choosing
p
such that
2/p-1/2=\delta
, the bound (
3.3
) follows.
∎

As a consequence of Lemma
3.2
, we have

Corollary 3.3
.

With the notations and hypothesis in Proposition
2.8
, we have

(3.7)

\sum_{|b|+|c|\leq|a|}\lVert\nabla\phi^{(b)}\nabla\phi^{(c)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{2}\min\{\langle t\rangle^{-1+4\delta},\langle t\rangle^{-1+(2+|a|)\delta}\}.

Proof.

We first consider the case of
|a|\geq N_{1}-1
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
|b|\leq|c|
. By (
2.10
) and (
3.2
) we have

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla\phi^{(b)}\nabla\phi^{(c)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k}2^{N(a)k^{+}}\lVert P_{k}\nabla\phi^{(b)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\nabla\phi^{(c)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta+H(|b|+2)\delta+H(c)\delta}\sum_{k}2^{\tfrac{k^{-}}{2}+N(a)k^{+}-N(|b|+1)k^{+}+(\tfrac{h}{2}+2)k^{+}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+4\delta}.

When
|a|\leq N_{1}-2
, by (
2.11
) and (
3.2
), we have

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla\phi^{(b)}\nabla\phi^{(c)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla\phi^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\lVert\nabla\phi^{(c)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\lVert\nabla\phi^{(b)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\nabla\phi^{(c)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{H(b)\delta-1+\delta+H(|c|+2)\delta}+\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta+H(|b|+2)\delta+H(b)\delta},

then we obtain

\lVert\nabla\phi^{(b)}\nabla\phi^{(c)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\lesssim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+4\delta},\ \ {\rm for\ }|a|=N_{1}-2,\\
&\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+(2+|a|)\delta},\ \ {\rm for\ }|a|<N_{1}-2,\end{aligned}\right.

which is acceptable. This concludes the bound (
3.7
).
∎

3.2.
Basic decay estimates of density
{\varrho}
and velocity field
u

Before proving the decay estimates of
{\varrho}
and
u
, we need the following bilinear estimates.

Lemma 3.4

(Bilinear estimates)
.

Under the bootstrap assumption (
2.21
), let
G({\varrho},\phi)
be smooth function, and
|\partial_{{\varrho}}^{k}\partial_{\phi}^{l}G|\leq C_{k,l}
for
k+l\leq N
,
4\leq N\leq N(0)
, let
f
and
g
be Schwartz functions,
\gamma\geq 1
, we have

(3.8)

\displaystyle\lVert G({\varrho},\phi)fg\rVert_{W^{N,1}}\lesssim\lVert f\rVert_{H^{N}}\lVert g\rVert_{H^{N}},

(3.9)

\displaystyle\lVert G({\varrho},\phi)fg\rVert_{H^{N}}\lesssim\lVert fg\rVert_{H^{N}}\lesssim\lVert f\rVert_{H^{N}}\lVert g\rVert_{H^{N}},

(3.10)

\displaystyle\lVert\big((1+{\varrho})^{\gamma-2}-1\big)\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{W^{N,1}\cap H^{N}}\lesssim\lVert{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}}\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}},

(3.11)

\displaystyle\lVert\big((1+{\varrho})^{\gamma-2}-1\big)\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}}\lesssim\lVert{\varrho}\rVert_{W^{N/2+1,\infty}}\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}}.

Proof.

The bound (
3.8
) and (
3.9
) are obtained by Hölder, Sobolev embedding, and the bound

\sum_{|n|\leq N/2}\lVert\partial^{n}G({\varrho},\phi)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\sum_{N/2<|n|\leq N}\lVert\partial^{n}G({\varrho},\phi)\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim(1+\lVert{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}}+\lVert\phi\rVert_{H^{N}})^{N}\lesssim 1,

which follows from Sobolev embedding,
|\partial_{{\varrho}}^{k}\partial_{\phi}^{l}G|\leq C_{k,l}
, and (
2.21
).

Next, we prove (
3.10
) and (
3.11
). By the equality

(1+{\varrho})^{\gamma-2}-1=(\gamma-2)\int_{0}^{{\varrho}}(1+s)^{\gamma-3}ds

and (
2.21
), we get

\lVert(1+{\varrho})^{\gamma-2}-1\rVert_{L^{p}}\lesssim\lVert{\varrho}\rVert_{L^{p}}\lVert(1+|{\varrho}|)^{\gamma-3}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\lVert{\varrho}\rVert_{L^{p}},\ {\rm for}\ 1\leq p\leq\infty.

Moreover, by Sobolev embedding and (
2.21
) we have

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq|n|\leq N}\lVert\partial^{n}[(1+{\varrho})^{\gamma-2}-1]\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq|n|\leq N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}+\cdots+n_{s}=n,\\
|n_{i}|\geq 1\end{subarray}}\lVert(1+{\varrho})^{\gamma-2-s}\partial^{n_{1}}{\varrho}\cdots\partial^{n_{s}}{\varrho}\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N-1}}+\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N-1}}^{N}\lesssim\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N-1}},

and

\sum_{1\leq|n|\leq N/2}\lVert\partial^{n}[(1+{\varrho})^{\gamma-2}-1]\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\lVert{\varrho}\rVert_{W^{N/2,\infty}}+\lVert{\varrho}\rVert_{W^{N/2,\infty}}^{N/2}\lesssim\lVert{\varrho}\rVert_{W^{N/2,\infty}}.

Then the bounds (
3.10
) and (
3.11
) follow by Hölder, Sobolev embedding, and the above three bounds.

∎

We then prove various bounds on the nonlinearities
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
. Indeed the nonlinearities
F_{2}
can be rewritten as

F_{2}=F_{2,I}+F_{2,II},

where

\displaystyle F_{2,I}:=

\displaystyle-u\cdot\nabla u+(a\gamma-\frac{P^{\prime}(1+{\varrho})}{1+{\varrho}})\nabla{\varrho}-\frac{{\varrho}}{1+{\varrho}}(\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}\Delta u+(\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}+\mu_{2})\nabla\mathrm{div}u)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(1+{\varrho})^{2}}[\frac{1}{2}\nabla{\varrho}(\cos^{2}\phi_{2}|\nabla\phi_{1}|^{2}+|\nabla\phi_{2}|^{2})-\partial_{j}{\varrho}(\cos^{2}\phi_{2}\nabla\phi_{1}\partial_{j}\phi_{1}+\nabla\phi_{2}\partial_{j}\phi_{2})],

\displaystyle F_{2,II}:=

\displaystyle\nabla[\frac{1}{2(1+{\varrho})}(\cos^{2}\phi_{2}|\nabla\phi_{1}|^{2}+|\nabla\phi_{2}|^{2})]-\partial_{j}[\frac{1}{1+{\varrho}}(\cos^{2}\phi_{2}\nabla\phi_{1}\partial_{j}\phi_{1}+\nabla\phi_{2}\partial_{j}\phi_{2})].

Corollary 3.5
.

Under the bootstrap assumptions (
2.21
) and (
2.22
), for any
N(0)-3\leq N\leq N(0)-1
,
t\in[0,T]
, we have

(3.12)

\displaystyle\lVert F_{1}\rVert_{W^{N,1}}+\lVert F_{2,I}\rVert_{W^{N,1}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}(\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}}+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N+1}}),

(3.13)

\displaystyle\lVert F_{1}\rVert_{H^{N}}+\lVert F_{2,I}\rVert_{H^{N}}\lesssim(\lVert({\varrho},u)\rVert_{W^{N(0)-4,\infty}}+\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+2\delta})(\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}}+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N+1}}),

(3.14)

\displaystyle\lVert F_{2,II}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}\lesssim\lVert\nabla\phi\rVert_{H^{N(0)}}\lVert\nabla\phi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+2\delta}.

Proof.

In view of
F_{1}
,
F_{2,I}
and
F_{2,II}
, the Corollary is an consequence of Lemma
3.4
, (
3.2
) and the bootstrap assumption (
2.21
).

For the first two bounds, we only estimate the following two terms in detail

\tfrac{{\varrho}}{1+{\varrho}}\nabla^{2}u,\ \ \ \ \tfrac{1}{(1+{\varrho})^{2}}\nabla{\varrho}(\cos^{2}\phi_{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}).

because other terms can be estiamted similarly. By (
3.8
) and (
2.21
), we have

\lVert\tfrac{1}{1+{\varrho}}{\varrho}\nabla^{2}u\rVert_{W^{N,1}}\lesssim\lVert{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}}\lVert\nabla^{2}u\rVert_{H^{N}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N+1}},

and by (
3.9
) and (
2.11
) we have

\lVert\tfrac{1}{1+{\varrho}}{\varrho}\nabla^{2}u\rVert_{H^{N}}\lesssim\lVert{\varrho}\nabla^{2}u\rVert_{H^{N}}\lesssim\lVert{\varrho}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\nabla^{2}u\rVert_{H^{N}}+\lVert{\varrho}\rVert_{\dot{H}^{N}}\lVert\nabla^{2}u\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\lVert({\varrho},u)\rVert_{W^{2,\infty}}(\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}}+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N+1}}).

These are acceptable for (
3.12
) and (
3.13
). From (
3.8
), (
3.9
) and (
3.2
), we can also bound the second term by

\displaystyle\lVert\tfrac{1}{(1+{\varrho})^{2}}\nabla{\varrho}(\cos^{2}\phi_{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2})\rVert_{W^{N,1}\cap H^{N}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}}\lVert\cos^{2}\phi_{2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}\rVert_{H^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}}\lVert|\nabla\phi|^{2}\rVert_{H^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}}\lVert\nabla\phi\rVert_{H^{N}}\lVert\nabla\phi\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+2\delta}\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N}},

which is acceptable for (
3.12
) and (
3.13
).

Finally, the bound (
3.14
) can be easily obtained by (
3.9
), (
2.11
), and (
3.2
). This completes the proof of the lemma.

∎

As a consequence of Corollary
3.5
and linear decay estimates (
2.4
), (
2.5
), and (
2.6
) we get the following decay estimates of
{\varrho},v
, and
w
.

Lemma 3.6
.

With the hypothesis in Proposition
2.8
, let
p=\frac{3}{2\delta}
, for any
t\in[0,T]
, we have

(3.15)

\displaystyle\lVert({\varrho},v,w)(t)\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-3/4+\delta},

(3.16)

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla({\varrho},v,w)\rVert_{H^{N(0)-2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-1/2},

(3.17)

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla^{2}({\varrho},v,w)\rVert_{H^{N(0)-5}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-1+2\delta}.

Proof.

For
0\leq t\leq 1
, (
3.15
)-(
3.17
) are the consequences of Sobolev embedding and (
2.21
). Now it suffices to prove the bounds (
3.15
)-(
3.17
) for
t>1
.

Proof of (
3.15
) for
t>1
.
From (
1.14
), it suffices to prove

(3.18)

\lVert e^{tB}({\varrho}_{0},v_{0})^{\top}\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}}+\lVert e^{t\Delta}w_{0}\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}t^{-3/4+\delta}

and

(3.19)

\displaystyle\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)B}(F_{1},\frac{\nabla\cdot F_{2}}{|\nabla|})^{\top}ds\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}}+\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Delta}{\mathbb{P}}F_{2}ds\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-1+2\delta}+\epsilon_{1}\sup_{s\in[t/2,t]}\lVert({\varrho},v,w)(s)\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}},

then, by Duhamel’s formula and (
3.18
) and (
3.19
) we get

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[1,T]}\langle t\rangle^{3/4-\delta}\lVert({\varrho},v,w)\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{1}\sup_{t\in[1,T]}\langle t\rangle^{3/4-\delta}\lVert({\varrho},v,w)\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}},

which implies (
3.15
) immediately.

Now we prove the bounds (
3.18
) and (
3.19
), respectively. The bound (
3.18
) follows from (
2.12
), (
2.5
), (
2.4
) and (
1.11
). (
3.19
) can be divided into two parts. By (
2.5
) and (
2.4
), we have

\displaystyle I_{[0,\tfrac{t}{2}]}:=

\displaystyle\lVert\int_{0}^{t/2}e^{(t-s)B}(F_{1},\frac{\nabla\cdot F_{2}}{|\nabla|})^{\top}+e^{(t-s)\Delta}{\mathbb{P}}F_{2}ds\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}(t-s)^{-3/2+\delta}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2}^{I})\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,1}}+e^{-c(t-s)}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}

\displaystyle+(t-s)^{-2+\delta}\lVert\frac{1+\cos^{2}\phi_{2}}{1+{\varrho}}\nabla\phi\nabla\phi\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,1}}ds.

Then (
3.12
), (
3.13
) and (
3.8
) imply

(3.20)

\displaystyle I_{[0,\tfrac{t}{2}]}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}(t-s)^{-3/2+\delta}\epsilon_{1}(\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N(0)}})+e^{-c(t-s)}(\epsilon_{1}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N(0)}})+\epsilon_{1}^{2}(t-s)^{-2+\delta}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-1+\delta}.

For the other part, by (
2.12
), (
2.5
), (
2.4
) and (
2.1
), we have

\displaystyle I_{[\tfrac{t}{2},t]}:=

\displaystyle\lVert\int_{t/2}^{t}e^{(t-s)B}(F_{1},\frac{\nabla\cdot F_{2}}{|\nabla|})^{\top}+e^{(t-s)\Delta}{\mathbb{P}}F_{2}ds\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{t/2}^{t-1}(t-s)^{-3/4+\delta}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2}^{I})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}+(t-s)^{-5/4+5\delta/3}\lVert\frac{1+\cos^{2}\phi_{2}}{1+{\varrho}}\nabla\phi\nabla\phi\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}

\displaystyle+e^{-c(t-s)}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}ds+\int_{t-1}^{t}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}ds

Then by (
3.13
), (
3.14
) and (
2.21
), we get

(3.21)

\displaystyle I_{[\tfrac{t}{2},t]}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{t/2}^{t-1}(t-s)^{-3/4+\delta}(\lVert({\varrho},u)\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}}+\epsilon_{1}^{2}s^{-1+2\delta})(\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N(0)}})

\displaystyle+(t-s)^{-5/4+5\delta/3}\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle s\rangle^{-1+2\delta}+e^{-c(t-s)}[\lVert({\varrho},u)\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}}(\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N(0)}})+\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle s\rangle^{-1+2\delta}]ds

\displaystyle+\int_{t-1}^{t}\lVert({\varrho},u)\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}}(\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N(0)}})+\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle s\rangle^{-1+2\delta}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\sup_{s\in[t/2,t]}\lVert({\varrho},u)(s)\rVert_{W^{N(0)-3,p}}+\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+2\delta}.

Hence, the desired bound (
3.19
) follows from (
3.20
) and (
3.21
). Thus the proof of (
3.15
) completes.

Proof of (
3.16
) for
t>1
.
By Duhamel’s formula, it suffices to prove that

(3.22)

\lVert e^{tB}|\nabla|({\varrho}_{0},v_{0})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-2}}+\lVert e^{t\Delta}|\nabla|w_{0}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}t^{-1/2},

and

(3.23)

\int_{0}^{t}\lVert e^{(t-s)B}(|\nabla|F_{1},\nabla\cdot F_{2})^{\top}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-2}}+\lVert e^{(t-s)\Delta}|\nabla|{\mathbb{P}}F_{2}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-2}}ds\lesssim\epsilon_{1}t^{-3/4+2\delta}.

The bound (
3.22
) follows by (
2.6
), (
2.4
) and (
1.11
). Thus we only show (
3.23
). Using (
2.6
), (
3.8
) and (
3.9
), it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}\lVert e^{(t-s)B}(|\nabla|F_{1},\nabla\cdot F_{2})^{\top}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-2}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}(t-s)^{-5/4}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2}^{I})\rVert_{W^{N(0)-2,1}}+(t-s)^{-7/4}\lVert\nabla\phi\rVert_{H^{N(0)-2}}^{2}+e^{-c(t-s)}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}ds,

and

\displaystyle\int_{t/2}^{t}\lVert e^{(t-s)B}(|\nabla|F_{1},\nabla\cdot F_{2})^{\top}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-2}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{t/2}^{t}\langle t-s\rangle^{-1/2}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2}^{I})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}+\langle t-s\rangle^{-1}\lVert\nabla\phi\rVert_{H^{N(0)}}^{2}+e^{-c(t-s)}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}ds.

Then by (
3.12
), (
3.13
), (
3.14
), (
3.15
), Hölder and (
2.21
) we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\lVert e^{(t-s)B}(|\nabla|F_{1},\nabla\cdot F_{2})^{\top}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-2}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}(t-s)^{-5/4}\epsilon_{1}(\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-2}}+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}})+(t-s)^{-7/4}\epsilon_{1}^{2}+e^{-c(t-s)}(\epsilon_{1}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N(0)}})ds

\displaystyle+\int_{t/2}^{t}\Big[\langle t-s\rangle^{-1/2}\epsilon_{1}s^{-3/4+\delta}(\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N(0)}})+\langle t-s\rangle^{-1}\epsilon_{1}^{2}s^{-1+2\delta}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +e^{-c(t-s)}(\epsilon_{1}s^{-3/4+\delta}(\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}}+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N(0)}})+\epsilon_{1}^{2}s^{-1+2\delta})\Big]ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-3/4}+\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-2}+\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-3/4+2\delta}+\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-1+3\delta}+\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-3/4}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-3/4+2\delta}.

Hence, the proof of (
3.16
) for
{\varrho},v
is obtained. Using similar argument, we can also get the bound (
3.16
) for
w
. This completes the proof of (
3.16
).

Proof of (
3.17
) for
t>1
.
By Duhamel’s formula, it suffices to prove

(3.24)

\displaystyle\lVert e^{tB}\nabla^{2}({\varrho}_{0},v_{0})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-5}}+\lVert e^{t\Delta}\nabla^{2}w_{0}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-5}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}t^{-1},

(3.25)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\lVert e^{(t-s)B}\nabla^{2}(F_{1},|\nabla|^{-1}\nabla\cdot F_{2})^{\top}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-5}}+\lVert e^{(t-s)\Delta}\nabla^{2}{\mathbb{P}}F_{2}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-5}}ds\lesssim\epsilon_{1}t^{-1+2\delta}.

From (
2.6
), (
2.4
) and (
1.11
), we obtain (
3.24
). For (
3.25
), by (
2.6
) and (
2.4
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{[0,\tfrac{t}{2}]}:=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}\lVert e^{(t-s)B}\nabla^{2}(F_{1},|\nabla|^{-1}\nabla\cdot F_{2})^{\top}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-5}}+\lVert e^{(t-s)\Delta}\nabla^{2}{\mathbb{P}}F_{2}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-5}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}(t-s)^{-7/4}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2,I})\rVert_{W^{N(0)-5,1}}+(t-s)^{-9/4}\lVert\nabla\phi\rVert_{H^{N(0)}}^{2}+e^{-c(t-s)}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-3}}ds.

Thus by Corollary
3.5
, Hölder and (
2.21
) we get

(3.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{[0,\tfrac{t}{2}]}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t/2}\epsilon_{1}t^{-7/4}(\lVert\nabla{\varrho}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-2}}+\lVert\nabla u\rVert_{H^{N(0)-1}})+\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-9/4}+\epsilon_{1}^{2}e^{-c(t-s)}ds\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-5/4}.

Next, by (
2.6
) and (
2.4
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{[\tfrac{t}{2},t]}:=

\displaystyle\int_{t/2}^{t}\lVert e^{(t-s)B}\nabla^{2}(F_{1},|\nabla|^{-1}\nabla\cdot F_{2})^{\top}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-5}}+\lVert e^{(t-s)\Delta}\nabla^{2}{\mathbb{P}}F_{2}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-5}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{t/2}^{t}\langle t-s\rangle^{-1}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2,I})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-3}}+\langle t-s\rangle^{-3/2}\lVert(\nabla\phi)^{2}\rVert_{H^{N(0)-2}}+e^{-c(t-s)}\lVert(F_{1},F_{2})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-3}}ds.

Since (
3.13
), (
3.15
) and (
3.16
) give

\displaystyle\lVert(F_{1},F_{2,I})\rVert_{H^{N(0)-3}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-5/4+\delta},

by (
3.14
) and (
3.2
) we can bound the left hand side of (
3.2
) by

(3.27)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{[\tfrac{t}{2},t]}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{t/2}^{t}\langle t-s\rangle^{-1}\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle s\rangle^{-5/4+\delta}+\langle t-s\rangle^{-3/2}\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle s\rangle^{-1+2\delta}+e^{-c(t-s)}\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+2\delta}ds\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+2\delta}.

Hence, (
3.26
) and (
3.27
) imply the bound (
3.25
). This completes the proof of (
3.17
). ∎

We prove now the decay estimates of
{\varrho}^{(a)},u^{(a)}
for
1\leq|a|\leq N_{1}
by induction. From the system (
2.19
), we rewrite the nonlinearities
\tilde{F}_{2}^{(a)}
as

\tilde{F}_{2}^{(a)}=\tilde{F}_{2,I}^{(a)}+\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)}+\tilde{F}_{2,III}^{(a)}+(S+1)^{a_{1}}\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}Err2,

where

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{2,I}^{(a)}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{a_{1}}C_{a_{1}}^{l}(-1)^{l}\big(\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}\Delta S^{a_{1}-l}\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}u+(\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}+\mu_{2})\nabla\mathrm{div}S^{a_{1}-l}\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}u\big),

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)}:=

\displaystyle(a\gamma-\frac{P^{\prime}(1+{\varrho})}{1+{\varrho}})\nabla{\varrho}^{(a)}-\sum_{b+c=a}C^{b}_{a}Z^{b}u\cdot\nabla Z^{c}u-\sum_{b+c=a,|b|\geq 1}C^{b}_{a}Z^{b}\Big(\frac{P^{\prime}(1+{\varrho})}{1+{\varrho}}\Big)\nabla Z^{c}{\varrho}

\displaystyle+\sum_{b+c=a}C^{b}_{a}\nabla Z^{b}\Big(\frac{{\varrho}}{1+{\varrho}}\Big)\Big[\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}\nabla(S-1)^{(c_{1})}\Gamma^{(c^{\prime})}u+(\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}+\mu_{2})\mathrm{div}(S-1)^{c_{1}}\Gamma^{c^{\prime}}u\Big]

\displaystyle-\sum_{b+c+e=a}C^{b,c}_{a}\Big[\frac{1}{2}\nabla Z^{b}\Big(\frac{1}{1+{\varrho}}\Big)\big(\nabla(S-1)^{c_{1}}\Gamma^{c^{\prime}}\phi\cdot\nabla(S-1)^{e_{1}}\Gamma^{e^{\prime}}\phi\big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ -\partial_{j}Z^{b}\Big(\frac{1}{1+{\varrho}}\Big)\big(\nabla(S-1)^{c_{1}}\Gamma^{c^{\prime}}\phi\cdot\partial_{j}(S-1)^{e_{1}}\Gamma^{e^{\prime}}\phi\big)\Big],

and

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{2,III}^{(a)}:=

\displaystyle-\sum_{b+c=a}C^{b}_{a}\nabla\Big[Z^{b}\Big(\frac{{\varrho}}{1+{\varrho}}\Big)\Big(\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}\nabla(S-1)^{(c_{1})}\Gamma^{(c^{\prime})}u+(\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}+\mu_{2})\mathrm{div}Z^{b}\Big(\frac{{\varrho}}{1+{\varrho}}\Big)\mathrm{div}(S-1)^{c_{1}}\Gamma^{c^{\prime}}u\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{b+c+e=a}C^{b,c}_{a}\Big[\frac{1}{2}\nabla\Big(Z^{b}\Big(\frac{1}{1+{\varrho}}\Big)\nabla(S-1)^{c_{1}}\Gamma^{c^{\prime}}\phi\cdot\nabla(S-1)^{e_{1}}\Gamma^{e^{\prime}}\phi\Big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ -\partial_{j}\Big(Z^{b}\big(\frac{1}{1+{\varrho}}\big)\cdot\nabla(S-1)^{c_{1}}\Gamma^{c^{\prime}}\phi\cdot\partial_{j}(S-1)^{e_{1}}\Gamma^{e^{\prime}}\phi\Big)\Big].

Lemma 3.7
.

Under the bootstrap assumptions (
2.21
) and (
2.22
), for any
N(a)-3\leq N\leq N(a)-1
,
t\in[0,T]
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{F}_{1}^{(a)}\rVert_{W^{N,1}}+\lVert\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)}\rVert_{W^{N,1}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\sum_{|b|\leq|a|}(\lVert\nabla{\varrho}^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N}}+\lVert\nabla u^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N}}),

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{F}_{1}^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N}}+\lVert\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert({\varrho}^{(b)},u^{(b)})\rVert_{W^{N(a)/2,\infty}}\lVert(\nabla{\varrho}^{(c)},\nabla u^{(c)})\rVert_{H^{N}}

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+5\delta}\lVert\nabla{\varrho}^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N}}.

Lemma 3.8
.

With the hypothesis in Proposition
2.8
, let
p=\frac{3}{2\delta}
, for any
t\in[0,T]
, we have

(3.28)

\displaystyle\lVert({\varrho}^{(a)},v^{(a)},w^{(a)})(t)\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-3/4+(2+|a|)\delta},\ \ \ {\rm for}\ 1\leq|a|\leq N_{1}.

(3.29)

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla({\varrho}^{(a)},v^{(a)},w^{(a)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-1/2+(2+|a|)\delta},

(3.30)

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla^{2}({\varrho}^{(a)},v^{(a)},w^{(a)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)-5}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-1+(2+|a|)\delta}.

Proof.

We prove the bounds (
3.28
)-(
3.30
) by induction. Precisely, by Lemma
3.6
, we may assume that the bounds (
3.28
)-(
3.30
) hold for
|a|\leq l-1\leq N_{1}-1
, then we prove (
3.28
)-(
3.30
) for
|a|=l
. Now it suffices to prove these bounds for
t>1
, otherwise by (
2.1
) and (
2.21
) the bounds (
3.28
)-(
3.30
) for
t\leq 1
follow immediately.

Proof of (
3.28
) for
t>1
.

By Duhamel’s formula, it suffices to prove

(3.31)

\displaystyle\lVert e^{tB}({\varrho}^{(a)}_{0},v^{(a)}_{0})^{\top}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}+\lVert e^{t\Delta}w^{(a)}_{0}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}t^{-3/4+\delta},

(3.32)

\displaystyle\sum_{|b|\leq|a|-1}(\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)B}(0,\Delta u^{(b)})^{\top}ds\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}+\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Delta}{\mathbb{P}}\Delta u^{(b)}ds\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}})\lesssim\epsilon_{1}t^{-3/4+(2+|b|)\delta},

(3.33)

\displaystyle\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)B}(F_{1}^{(a)},\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)})^{\top}ds\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}+\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Delta}\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)}ds\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-3/4+(2+|a|)\delta}+\epsilon_{1}\sup_{s\in[t/2,t]}\lVert({\varrho}^{(a)},v^{(a)},w^{(a)})(s)\rVert_{W^{N(a)/2,p}},

(3.34)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\lVert e^{(t-s)B}(0,\tilde{F}_{2,III}^{(a)})^{\top}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}+\lVert e^{(t-s)\Delta}\tilde{F}_{2,III}^{(a)}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-3/4+(2+|a|)\delta}+\epsilon_{1}\sup_{s\in[t/2,t]}(\lVert({\varrho}^{(a)},u^{(a)})(s)\rVert_{W^{1,p}}),

and

(3.35)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\lVert e^{(t-s)B}(0,(S+1)^{a_{1}}\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}Err2)^{\top}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}+\lVert e^{(t-s)\Delta}(S+1)^{a_{1}}\Gamma^{a^{\prime}}Err2\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+(2+|a|)\delta}

Now we begin to prove the bounds (
3.31
)-(
3.35
), respectively. The bound (
3.31
) follows from (
2.5
), (
2.4
) and (
1.11
) directly.

Step 1. Proof of the bound (
3.32
).
By (
2.5
), (
2.4
) and inductive assumptions, we have for any
|b|\leq|a|-1

\displaystyle\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)B}(0,\Delta u^{(b)})^{\top}ds\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}+\lVert\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Delta}\Delta u^{(b)}ds\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}(t-s)^{-7/4+\delta}\lVert u^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)-3}}ds+\int_{t/2}^{t}\langle t-s\rangle^{-3/4+\delta}\lVert\nabla^{2}u^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)-1}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)}\lVert\nabla^{2}u^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)-1}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}t^{-3/4+\delta}+\epsilon_{1}t^{-1+(2+|b|)\delta}t^{1/4+\delta}+t^{-2}\int_{0}^{t/2}(t-s)^{2}e^{-c(t-s)}\epsilon_{1}ds+\int_{t/2}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)}\epsilon_{1}\langle s\rangle^{-1+(2+|b|)\delta}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-3/4+(2+|a|)\delta}.

Thus, the bound (
3.32
) follows.

Step 2. Proof of the bound (
3.33
).
By (
2.5
) and (
2.4
) we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}\lVert e^{(t-s)B}(F_{1}^{(a)},\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)})^{\top}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}+\lVert e^{(t-s)\Delta}\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}(t-s)^{-3/2+\delta}\lVert(F_{1}^{(a)},\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)})\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,1}}+e^{-c(t-s)}\lVert(F_{1}^{(a)},\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)-1}}ds,

and

\displaystyle\int_{t/2}^{t}\lVert e^{(t-s)B}(F_{1}^{(a)},\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)})^{\top}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}+\lVert e^{(t-s)\Delta}\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{t/2}^{t}\langle t-s\rangle^{-3/4+\delta}\lVert(F_{1}^{(a)},\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)-1}}+e^{-c(t-s)}\lVert(F_{1}^{(a)},\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)-1}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{t/2}^{t}\langle t-s\rangle^{-3/4+\delta}\lVert(F_{1}^{(a)},\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)-1}}ds.

Then by Lemma
3.7
, inductive assumptions, Hölder and (
2.21
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\lVert e^{(t-s)B}(F_{1}^{(a)},\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)})^{\top}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}+\lVert e^{(t-s)\Delta}\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}\epsilon_{1}(t-s)^{-3/2+\delta}\sum_{|b|\leq|a|}\lVert\nabla({\varrho}^{(b)},u^{(b)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)-3}}+\epsilon_{1}e^{-c(t-s)}\sum_{|b|\leq|a|}\lVert(\nabla{\varrho}^{(b)},\nabla u^{(b)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)-1}\times H^{N(a)}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{t/2}^{t}\langle t-s\rangle^{-3/4+\delta}\big[(\epsilon_{1}\langle s\rangle^{-3/4+(2+|a|)\delta}+\lVert({\varrho}^{(a)},u^{(a)})\rVert_{W^{N(a)/2,\infty}})\lVert(\nabla{\varrho}^{(c)},\nabla u^{(c)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)-1}\times H^{N(a)}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle s\rangle^{-1+5\delta}\sum_{|b|\leq|a|}\lVert\nabla{\varrho}^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)-1}}\big]ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-1+\delta}+\epsilon_{1}(\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-3/4+(2+|a|)\delta}+\sup_{s\in[t/2,t]}\lVert({\varrho}^{(a)},u^{(a)})(s)\rVert_{W^{N(a)/2,p}})+\epsilon_{1}^{3}\langle t\rangle^{-1+5\delta}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-3/4+(2+|a|)\delta}+\epsilon_{1}\sup_{s\in[t/2,t]}\lVert({\varrho}^{(a)},u^{(a)})(s)\rVert_{W^{N(a)/2,p}}.

which yields the bounds (
3.33
).

Step 3. Proof of the bound (
3.34
) and (
3.35
).
By the definition of
\tilde{F}_{2,III}^{(a)}
, we only estimate for any
|b|+|c|\leq|a|
in detail

(3.36)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\lVert e^{(t-s)B}(0,\nabla({\varrho}^{(b)}\nabla u^{(c)}+\nabla\phi^{(b)}\nabla\phi^{(c)}))^{\top}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}

\displaystyle+\lVert e^{(t-s)\Delta}\nabla({\varrho}^{(b)}\nabla u^{(c)}+\nabla\phi^{(b)}\nabla\phi^{(c)})\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,p}}\Big)ds

From (
2.5
) and (
2.4
), we have

\displaystyle(\ref{F2IIIa-Dec})\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}(t-s)^{-2+\delta}\lVert{\varrho}^{(b)}\nabla u^{(c)}+\nabla\phi^{(b)}\nabla\phi^{(c)}\rVert_{W^{N(a)-3,1}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{t/2}^{t}\langle t-s\rangle^{-5/4+\delta}\lVert{\varrho}^{(b)}\nabla u^{(c)}+\nabla\phi^{(b)}\nabla\phi^{(c)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)}\lVert{\varrho}^{(b)}\nabla u^{(c)}+\nabla\phi^{(b)}\nabla\phi^{(c)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}ds.

Then by (
2.11
) and (
3.7
) we get

\displaystyle(\ref{F2IIIa-Dec})\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-1+4\delta}+\int_{t/2}^{t}\langle t-s\rangle^{-5/4+\delta}(\lVert{\varrho}^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\lVert\nabla u^{(c)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\lVert{\varrho}^{(b)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\nabla u^{(c)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+\epsilon_{1}\langle s\rangle^{-1+5\delta})ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)}(\lVert{\varrho}^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}\lVert\nabla u^{(c)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\lVert{\varrho}^{(b)}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert\nabla u^{(c)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)}}+\epsilon_{1}\langle s\rangle^{-1+5\delta})ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-1+5\delta}+\epsilon_{1}\sum_{|b|\leq|a|}\sup_{s\in[t/2,t]}(\lVert\nabla u^{(b)}(s)\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\lVert{\varrho}^{(b)}(s)\rVert_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-3/4+(2+|a|)\delta}+\epsilon_{1}\sup_{s\in[t/2,t]}(\lVert u^{(a)}(s)\rVert_{W^{2,p}}+\lVert{\varrho}^{(a)}(s)\rVert_{W^{2,p}}).

Hence the bound (
3.34
) follows. Using the similar argument to (
3.34
), we obtain (
3.35
). This completes the proof of (
3.28
).

Proof of (
3.29
) for
t>1
.

By Duhamel’s formula, it suffices to prove

(3.37)

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla e^{tB}({\varrho}^{(a)}_{0},v^{(a)}_{0})^{\top}\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}+\lVert\nabla e^{t\Delta}w^{(a)}_{0}\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}t^{-1/2},

(3.38)

\displaystyle\sum_{|b|\leq|a|-1}(\lVert\int_{0}^{t}\nabla e^{(t-s)B}(0,\Delta u^{(b)})^{\top}ds\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}+\lVert\int_{0}^{t}\nabla e^{(t-s)\Delta}\Delta u^{(b)}ds\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}})\lesssim\epsilon_{1}t^{-1/2+(2+|b|)\delta},

(3.39)

\displaystyle\lVert\int_{0}^{t}\nabla e^{(t-s)B}(F_{1}^{(a)},\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)})^{\top}ds\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}+\lVert\int_{0}^{t}\nabla e^{(t-s)\Delta}{\mathbb{P}}\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)}ds\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{2}t^{-3/4+(3+|a|)\delta},

(3.40)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla e^{(t-s)B}(0,\tilde{F}_{2,III}^{(a)})^{\top}\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}+\lVert\nabla e^{(t-s)\Delta}\tilde{F}_{2,III}^{(a)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}ds\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-3/4+5\delta},

and

(3.41)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla e^{(t-s)B}(0,Z^{a}Err2)^{\top}\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}+\lVert\nabla e^{(t-s)\Delta}Z^{a}Err2\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}ds\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+(2+|a|)\delta}.

Now we begin to prove the bounds (
3.37
)-(
3.41
), respectively. The bound (
3.37
) follows from (
2.6
), (
2.4
) and (
1.11
) directly.

Step 1. Proof of the bound (
3.38
).
By (
2.6
), (
2.4
), (
2.21
) and inductive assumptions, we have for any
|b|\leq|a|-1

\displaystyle\lVert\int_{0}^{t}\nabla e^{(t-s)B}(0,\Delta u^{(b)})^{\top}ds\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}+\lVert\int_{0}^{t}\nabla e^{(t-s)\Delta}\Delta u^{(b)}ds\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}(t-s)^{-3/2}\lVert u^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}ds+\int_{t/2}^{t}\langle t-s\rangle^{-1/2}\lVert\nabla^{2}u^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)-1}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)}\lVert\nabla^{3}u^{(b)}\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}t^{-1/2}+\epsilon_{1}t^{1/2-1+(2+|b|)\delta}+\epsilon_{1}t^{-2}\int_{0}^{t/2}(t-s)^{2}e^{-c(t-s)}ds+\epsilon_{1}t^{-1+(2+|b|)\delta}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}t^{-1/2+(2+|b|)\delta}.

Step 2. Proof of the bound (
3.39
).
By (
2.6
), (
2.4
) we have

(3.42)

\displaystyle\lVert\int_{0}^{t}\nabla e^{(t-s)B}(F_{1}^{(a)},\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)})^{\top}ds\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}+\lVert\int_{0}^{t}\nabla e^{(t-s)\Delta}{\mathbb{P}}\tilde{F}_{2,II}^{(a)}ds\rVert_{H^{N(a)-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t/2}(t-s)^{-5/4}\lVert(F_{1}^{(a)},F_{2,II}^{(a)})\rVert_{W^{N(a)-2,1}}ds+\int_{t/2}^{t}\langle t-s\rangle^{-1/2}\lVert(F_{1}^{(a)},F_{2,II}^{(a)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)-1}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)}\lVert(F_{1}^{(a)},F_{2,II}^{(a)})\rVert_{H^{N(a)-1}}ds.

From Lemma
3.7
, (
3.28
) and (
2.21
), we may bound this by

\displaystyle{\rm LHS}(\ref{drr,u-1/2_F1a})\lesssim
